Idea: Minimize max c via "balancing" algorithm:
- Start with p normalized.
- While time:
  - Compute convolution c via FFT and find max lag k*.
  - To reduce c_{k*}, adjust p by moving mass from pairs contributing to c_{k*} to positions not contributing? Specifically c_k =∑ p_i p_{k-i}. To reduce, decrease high p_i and p_{k-i}. But must preserve sum. Could use coordinate descent: pick pair (i,j) with i+j=k and large product, reduce one (and increase another not in active lags). Hard.
- Or use LP subproblem from SoA: Given current p, M=cmax. Solve max sum b subject p*b ≤ M. Then linear interpolate. This moves mass to positions where constraints allow. This is like trying to find new p with low max. We can implement subproblem via multiplicative weights/direct for moderate n.

LP subproblem details: p fixed nonnegative sum1. M = max(p*p). We want maximize sum b (then normalize) subject to b≥0, (p*b)_k ≤ M for k=0..n-1 maybe (or all 2n-1). We can solve LP approximately using projected gradient on dual:

def fast_convolve(p, free):
    n=len(p); N=1
    while N < 2*n-1: N*=2
    F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:2*n-1]
    return c
```
For gradient weights:
```
def gradient_from_weights(w, p):
    n=len(p); L=len(w); r=p[::-1]
    N=1
    while N < L+n-1: N*=2
    Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    Fr=np.fft.rfft(r,N)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(Fw*Fr,N)
    g=2*conv[n-1:n+n-1] ? Wait length: conv index i+n-1 for i=0..n-1. So conv[n-1:n+n-1]
    return g
```

Softmax weights:
```
def soft_weights(c, beta, smooth_params? active_tol):
   cmax = c.max()
   if beta < 1:
       # use far? 
   vals = c - cmax
   v = vals*beta
   # clip vals to avoid underflow
   weights = np.exp(np.clip(v, -50, 0))
   # optionally threshold small to reduce? 
   weights[weights < 1e-8] =0
   sumw=weights.sum()
   if sumw==0: weights = (c >= cmax)
   else weights /= sumw
   return weights
```
For exact active maybe use top_k:
```
def active_weights(c, top_fraction=0.05, eps=1e-3):
   cmax=c.max()
   threshold = cmax - max(eps*(cmax+1), 1e-12)
   w = (c>=threshold).astype(float)
   if w.sum()==0: w[np.argmax(c)]=1
   # Could weight linearly by positive part/(cmax-cmin)
   return w/w.sum()
```

Projected gradient step:
```
def project_simplex(v):
   v=np.asarray(v,float)
   n=v.size
   u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
   css=np.cumsum(u)
   rho=np.nonzero(u*(np.arange(1,n+1)-1) > (css-1))[0]  # sum >1? Standard: find rho where u_k - (sum_{i<=k} u_i -1)/k >0.
   if len(rho)==0: return np.zeros_like(v)
   lam=(css[rho[-1]]-1)/(rho[-1]+1)
   return np.maximum(v-lam,0)
```
For constraint sum=1 exactly, after project maybe sum 1. Use `p = project_simplex(p - step*g)`.

Binary anti search details:
- n even (e.g. 128, 256, 512, 1024). Represent first half bits as Python int of m bits. Full bitset B (2m bits) with anti second half.
- Compute R via FFT? For binary, c counts. We can represent p = 2/n * bits; score=8*Rmax/n. Use FFT of bits (int->float) to get R. Or use bit operations? FFT easy.
- Local search: flip a random pair of first-half bit and its complement (maintains anti, sum fixed). Evaluate score. Accept if improves. Use simulated annealing maybe. Many flips; evaluating FFT O(n log n) each. For n=256, very fast. We can also use incremental update of R? Maybe not necessary. 2400s could do thousands/millions.
- Better: Use exact objective direct for small n? np.convolve of int? Use FFT. For n=1024, evaluation ~0.1ms; millions maybe.
- To avoid local minima, use random restarts and Hill-climbing plus valley: allow occasional worse moves.
- Also use block flips: flip k positions at once (e.g., random masks of size 1-20) to jump.
- We can also optimize binary sequence using "simulated annealing" with schedule based on time: temperature T, accept if score<T. Score is F; improvements tiny integer steps. Use T from 0.1 down to 0.
- Could use best-biased: each iteration generate several mutations from current and global best; keep best.

Anti-box gradient optimization may be powerful when starting from binary. Let's implement an anti-palindromic optimizer:
- Variables u_i for first half, p first = α/2 + u_i, second = α/2 - u_{rev?}. α=2/n. Constraint |u_i|≤α/2. Sum auto. Objective F.
- Use gradient descent on box: u_norm = (1+u_i/(α/2))? Could optimize smoother. Gradient wrt u_i as earlier: grad_u_i = grad_p_i - grad_p_{N-1-i}. Use softmax weights. Project to box.
- Starting from anti binary: u=±α/2. Gradient descent can smooth values to reduce peaks. This may find continuous optimum. Good.

Let's design `local_search_anti(n, rng, start_v, time_limit)`:
- Initialize p from v: `p = np.empty(n); p[:m]=v; p[m:]=alpha-vrev?` Actually if v is first half length m, second half positions m..n-1 correspond i=m..n-1, rev index n-1-i. For n=2m, first half indices 0..m-1? Wait pairs are (0,n-1), (1,n-2),...,(m-1,m). If we choose first half as first m indices 0..m-1, their reverses are n-1 down to m. So v length m: p[i]=v[i], p[n-1-i]=α-v[i]. Then p order not simply first/second contiguous? The second half p[m:] will have values α - v[m-1-m?]. Let's create array.
```
def anti_from_v(v):
    n=2*v.size; alpha=2.0/n
    p=np.empty(n)
    p[:v.size]=v
    p[v.size:]=alpha - v[::-1]
    return p
```
Check n=8, v=[v0,v1,v2,v3]. p=[v0,v1,v2,v3, α-v3, α-v2, α-v1, α-v0]. Pair p0+p7=α, p1+p6=α, p2+p5=α, p3+p4=α. Good.
- Objective `score_seq(anti_from_v(v))`.
- If not finite, fallback v=α/2.
- For iterations:
  - `c=convolve(p,p)`, `cmax`, score.
  - If score update global/local.
  - Weights: `w = top_soft_weights(c, beta_factor=10, top_frac=0.03)`.
  - `g = gradient_full(w,p)`.
  - `grad_u = g[:m] - g[m:][::-1]?` p first indices 0..m-1; reverses indices n-1-i = m-1? For i=0..m-1, rev = n-1-i. In p array, order second half is p[m:] = α - v[::-1], so p[n-1-i] corresponds? For i=0 rev=7 p[7]=α-v0, i=1 rev=6. Thus `g_rev = g[n-1 : n-1-m : -1]` or simply `g[m-1::-1]`? Since n=2m, n-1-i for i=0..m-1 are 2m-1,2m-2,...,m. In 0-index slice `g[m-1::-1]` gives indices m-1? Wait for m=4, need g[7],g[6],g[5],g[4]. `g[4:8][::-1]` gives 7,6,5,4. So `g_rev = g[m:][::-1]`. Good. grad_u = g[:m] - g[m:][::-1].
  - `scale = np.linalg.norm(grad_u) / np.sqrt(m) + 1e-12`; direction = grad_u/scale.
  - Try steps: base = 0.1*alpha? But v scale maybe α/2. Use `base = 0.2*alpha` (10%? α/2 half amplitude=α/2, base 0.1α is 20% amplitude). for f in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125]: trial=clip(v - base*f*direction,0,α); score=fast_score(anti_from_v(trial)); accept first improvement over local best. If none, maybe accept small random perturbation if local stuck.
  - After every X iterations, if no improvement Y, restart from perturbed best or random within anti.
Need avoid too many score evals; each iteration up to 4 FFTs. Could be okay. Use `max_iters` based on time.

Let's define functions:

```
def fft_size(s):
    N=1
    while N < s: N <<=1
    return N
```

`convolve_same(p)`:
```
n=p.size
N=1
while N < 2*n-1: N <<=1
F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)
c = c[:2*n-1]
c[~np.isfinite(c)] = 0
return c
```

`score_from_p(p)`:
```
s=p.sum()
if s <=0: return inf
c=convolve_same(p/s?) Wait objective scale cancels; we can compute with p as is: 2n max(c)/(s*s). Normalizing not necessary. For gradients, better p sum1. But score invariant. In local search we maintain sum1 or anti auto sum1. For general, normalize.
```
Anti p from v automatically sum=1 (since pairs sum α, m pairs ->1). Good.

`gradient_for_weights(w,p)`:

r=p[::-1]
N=1
while N < (2*n-2)+(n-1): N <<=1 # 3n-3
Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
Fr=np.fft.rfft(r,N)
conv=np.fft.irfft(Fw*Fr,N)
g=np.zeros(n)
# need conv index n-1 .. n+n-2? length n
start=n-1
end=start+n
# conv may have tiny imaginary? irfft real
g = 2.0 * conv[start:end]
# Clean outside? If any nan set0
return g
```

`soft_weights(c, top_frac=0.02, beta_mult=10.0)`:
```
cmax=c.max()
# scale by range
cmin = np.min(c)
scale = max(cmax-cmin, 1e-12)
vals = (c - cmax) * (beta_mult / scale)
vals = np.clip(vals, -50, 0)
weights = np.exp(vals)
# zero very small to reduce? maybe not
threshold = np.exp(-12)
weights[weights < threshold] = 0
if weights.sum()<=0: weights = np.zeros_like(c); weights[np.argmax(c)]=1
# limit top count? If too many, keep highest by threshold:
# Sort? Avoid O(n log n) each iter. Could apply quantile threshold if top_frac small.
if c.size > 1000:
   # use threshold based on quantile
   q = 1.0 - top_frac
   thr = np.quantile(c, q)
   # use combined weight only where c >= thr? This focuses.
   weights *= (c >= thr)
   if weights.sum()==0: weights=...; weights[argmax]=1
weights /= weights.sum()
```

`general_local_search`:
```
def general_local_search(n, p0, rng, time_limit):
    p = normalize(p0)
    best_p=p.copy(); best=score
    no_improve=0
    while time<deadline:
       c=conv
       w=weights(c)
       g=grad
       # center gradient to sum zero? projection handles but for step scaling use g - g.mean()
       d = g - np.mean(g)
       scale = np.linalg.norm(d)/sqrt(n) + 1e-12
       base = 0.02  # absolute simplex movement
       accepted=False
       for f in [1,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.05]:
           trial = project_simplex(p - base*f*d/scale)
           s=score(trial)
           if s < best - 1e-12: accept
       if not accepted:
           # random perturbation
           trial = project_simplex(p + 0.01*(rng.normal(size=n)-0.5))
           s=score
           if s<best+0.05*temperature? accept anneal
       update
```
But general may be too slow. Use limited time.

- Binary random: v = choice [0,α] for first half.
- Smooth random: generate gaussian array length m, apply sinusoidal lowpass: `f = np.fft.rfft(x); f[idx]=f[idx]/(1+idx);` then anti? just use as smooth; normalize to range α/2.
- Ramps: q = linspace(0,1,m); create v=α/2 + amp*(q-0.5)/0.5 clipped.
- Power: q=(i/m)**gamma etc.
- Fourier anti: v_i=α/2 + α/2 * sin(2π k i/m + phase) maybe multiple.
- From height_sequence_1: Downsample/interpolate, enforce anti: given base p length L, resample to n: `q = np.interp(...)`; then `v = α/2 + 0.5*(q - q_rev)` clipped? For first half.

Let's also implement a direct "anneal anti" mutation:
```
def random_anti_mutation(v, rng, strength):
    m=v.size
    mut=v.copy()
    for _ in range(rng.integers(1,5)):
       w=int(rng.integers(1, max(2,m//10)))
       s=int(rng.integers(0,m-w))
       mut[s:s+w] = clip(mut[s:s+w]*rng.normal(1, strength) + rng.normal(0,strength*0.1),0,alpha)
    return mut
```
Accept if improved. This covers continuous changes.

- Trial = block mutation or gradient step. Accept if score < current + T. Keep best. But if using current for gradient, we can switch to best if best improves.
- Use `current = current` even if worse to explore.

Maybe simpler: Use a single multi-start anneal that doesn't separate methods:

Population algorithm:
```
def population_anti_search(n, pop_size=8, time_limit):
   npop = pop_size
   vs = list of starts
   scores = [score]
   best_v = argmin
   while time:
      for i in range(npop):
         v=vs[i]
         # occasionally restart worst with best blended
         r = rng.random()
         if r <0.2:
             # mutation: random smooth noise
             trial = random_anti_mutation(best_v, rng, strength decreasing)
         elif r < 0.6:
             trial = anti_gradient_step(v, rng)
         else:
             trial = interpolate(v,best_v, rng)
         score=fast_score(anti_from_v(clip(trial)))
         if score < scores[i] or anneal accept: replace
         if score < best: update
   return best
```
Gradient step may be expensive but one eval. Use no line search for speed: fixed step normalized, accept if better or temperature. Simulated annealing can escape. This is simple, vectorized? Each trial separate FFT. n=512 fast.

def anti_ize(p, n=None):
   if n None: n=p.size
   if n%2: n-=1? use even; if p length odd, take first even or interpolate.

Population anti search:
```
def population_anti_search(n, seed, time_limit, start_vs=None):
   m=n//2; alpha=2/n
   pop=[]
   if start_vs: include
   else generate 6 varied
   for v in pop: p=anti_from_v(v); s=fast_score(p); pop append [v,s,p]
   best...
   T=0.1; it=0
   while time left:
      # choose individual with some probability: best 0.5, others round robin
      i = rng.integers(len(pop)) if it%2==0 else argmin? Maybe round-robin to update all.
      v,s = pop[i]
      # choose mutation method
      r = rng.random()
      if r <0.35:
         trial = anti_gradient_trial(v, alpha, beta_mult, step, rng)
      elif r <0.65:
         trial = smooth_mutation(v, alpha, rng, strength=0.3)
      elif r <0.85:
         # blend with best
         trial = (1-lam)*v + lam*best_v + noise
      else:
         trial = random_anti_v(...)
      # maybe if trial nan, use v
      p=anti_from_v(clip); ts=fast_score(p)
      if not finite: continue
      # acceptance
      if ts < s or (ts < s + T):
          pop[i]=[trial,ts,p]
      if ts < best_s: best=...
      it +=1
      if it % 100: T *= 0.5? Better time-based: T=0.1 * max(0.01, remaining fraction^2) or anneal each 1000.
      # If population stagnate, restart worst with random or upsample best
      if no improvement for long: worst = max(s); pop[worst]=random start
```
Need avoid expensive weight/gradient for every trial (one FFT extra for gradient). `anti_gradient_trial` computes c, weights, gradient, new v, score (total 2 conv? c already; score uses c). We can reuse c? For each trial, we need score of trial; gradient uses current p. We can compute current c/score before, else pop has s. To compute gradient, need weights from current c. We didn't store c; we can recompute c for current (one FFT). Then trial score another FFT. So gradient trial 2 FFTs. Acceptable for n=512.

Set population size maybe 12. For n=256, very fast. For n=1024/2048, slower; use smaller pop and gradient less frequent.

Maybe use batch by running `score_many(vs, batch=16)` to evaluate random/mutations without individual FFT overhead. But gradient not batchable easily. We can implement for mutation trials: generate batch of Bs, compute convolutions vectorized, scores, then replace worst. This speeds exploration.

Vectorized convolution:
```
def score_many_p(p_batch): # shape (B,n)
   N=1 while N<2n-1
   F=np.fft.rfft(p_batch, axes=1)
   C=np.fft.irfft(F*F, axes=1)[:B, :2n-1]
   cmax=np.max(C, axis=1)
   sums=np.sum(p_batch, axis=1)
   scores=2*n*cmax/(sums*sums)
   return cmax, scores
```
But for anti p, sums=1. We can generate batch of trial v, convert to p batch, score. This helps random/mutation/blend. Could accept multiple improvements. We'll implement for mutation phase: each iteration generate B=16-64 trials, score vectorized, update population best. But if trial gradient high-level not. We can do one gradient trial plus a batch of random mutations per population individual? Might be overkill.

Simpler: Population search with batch of K from each individual: For each pop individual every cycle, generate K mutations (random smooth/block) and score batch; choose best to replace individual. Also do gradient step for best individual. This is efficient in Python due vectorized FFT.

Let's design `population_anti_search` with vectorized evaluations:
- Data structures: `pop_v` shape (P,m), `pop_s` length P.
- `evaluate_v_batch(vs)`: vs (B,m), build p (B,n): p[:, :m]=vs, p[:,m:]=alpha - vs[:, ::-1]? Wait need p second half = alpha - v[::-1] along last axis: `p[:, m:] = alpha - np.flip(vs, axis=1)`. Compute scores.
- Main loop (time): For each pop i (round-robin) or best:
   - Generate `B_mut` trials:
      * Random global: `smooth random v`
      * Local mutation: block perturb `pop_v[i]`
      * Blend with best: linear combination
   - Score batch. Replace `pop_i` with min among original and trials. Update best.
   - Additionally, if i==best or periodically, do gradient step for best: use current best p, compute c, weights, grad, direction, produce 4 step sizes trial and score; update.
- Temperature not needed if only accept improvements; but can keep anneal by replacing with random if no improvement. To escape, include random restarts in batch (e.g., 1/10 trials are new random even if worse) and restart worst after stagnation.

```
def anti_p_batch(vs, alpha):
    B=vs.shape[0]; m=vs.shape[1]; n=2*m
    p=np.empty((B,n), dtype=np.float64)
    p[:, :m]=vs
    p[:, m:] = alpha - np.flip(vs, axis=1)
    # clip? assume
    return p
```
But second half order: for v length m, p[m:] should be [α-v[m-1],...,α-v[0]] = flip. Good.

`score_anti_batch(vs)` calls anti_p_batch then `score_p_batch` with sum1 maybe due rounding. For speed, normalize? Values clipped [0,α], sum exactly? α float, zeros maybe sum mα=1 but due rounding, okay. Compute sums.

`score_p_batch(p)`:
```
def score_p_batch(p):
    B,n=p.shape
    N=1
    while N < 2*n-1: N <<=1
    F=np.fft.rfft(p, axis=1)
    C=np.fft.irfft(F*F, axis=1)[:, :2*n-1]
    cmax=np.max(C, axis=1)
    sums=np.sum(p, axis=1)
    with errstate: scores=2*n*cmax/(sums*sums)
    scores[~finite]=inf
    return cmax, scores
```
For single p, `score_p = float(score_p_batch(p[None])[1][0])`.

Vectorized gradient for single best still.

`generate_trial_batch(base_v, alpha, rng, count, strength=1.0)`:
```
B=count+1
m=base_v.size
trials=np.empty((B,m))
trials[0]=base_v
alpha_half=alpha/2
for i in range(1,B):
  t=base_v.copy()
  typ=rng.integers(0,4)
  if typ==0: # gaussian
      sigma=alpha*rng.uniform(0.02,0.15*strength)
      t += rng.normal(0, sigma, m)
  elif typ==1: # block scale
      width=max(1, int(m*rng.uniform(0.02,0.25)))
      start=int(rng.integers(0,m-width+1))
      t[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0, 0.2*strength))
  elif typ==2: # blend uniform/best
      lam=rng.uniform(0.1,0.5)
      t=(1-lam)*base_v + lam*(alpha/2) + small noise
  else:
      # local anti-feature: add low freq sine with random phase/amplitude
      amp=alpha*rng.uniform(0.05,0.5*strength)
      freq=int(rng.integers(1,max(2,m//10)))
      phase=rng.uniform(0,2π)
      idx=np.arange(m)
      t += amp*np.sin(2π*freq*idx/m + phase)
  np.clip(t,0,alpha,out=t)
trials = np.clip(trials,0,alpha)
return trials
```
This is top-level function; no closures. Need `m` etc.

Population search function:
```
def population_anti_search(n, rng, time_limit, initial_vs=None):
   m=n//2; alpha=2/n
   P=12
   pops=[]
   if initial_vs: add
   while len(pops)<P and time left:
       pops.append(random_anti_v(m,alpha,rng))
   vs=np.stack(pops)
   _, scores = score_anti_batch(vs)
   # handle inf => reset
   best_idx=argmin; best_v=vs[best_idx].copy(); best_score=scores[best_idx]
    no_improve=0
   trials_per=8
   while time left:
      # update one or two individuals round-robin; keep round index
      i = int(rng.integers(P)) # or cycle
      base=vs[i]
      # generate batch
      B = 12 if time left big else 4
      trial_vs=generate_trial_batch(base, alpha, rng, B, strength=0.5 + 0.5*min(1,no_improve/1000?) )
      # maybe include best blended with base
      lam=rng.uniform(0.2,0.8); trial_vs[-1]=(1-lam)*base+lam*best_v+noise
      _,trial_scores=score_anti_batch(trial_vs)
      if trial_scores.min() < scores[i]:
          j=int(np.argmin(trial_scores))
          vs[i]=trial_vs[j]; scores[i]=trial_scores[j]
          if scores[i] < best_score: update
          no_improve=0
      else:
          no_improve+=1
          if no_improve > 2000: # reset worst with random
              worst=int(np.argmax(scores))
              vs[worst]=random_anti_v(m,alpha,rng)
              _,sc=score_anti_batch(vs[worst][None]); scores[worst]=sc[0]
              if < best update
              no_improve=0
      # gradient step on best (or if no improve often)
      if (no_improve % 20 ==0) or (time since last grad >5):
          trial=anti_gradient_best(best_v, alpha, rng)
          if trial not None:
             sc=score; if sc<best_score: update; also set a pop to best
      # log if time
   return best_v, best_score
```

def anti_gradient_best(v, alpha, rng, beta_mult=8.0):
   p=anti_from_v(v)
   c=convolve_same(p)
   cmax=c.max()
   if cmax<=0: return None
   w=make_weights(c, beta_mult)
   g=gradient_from_weights(w,p)
   m=v.size
   d=g[:m]-g[m:][::-1]
   # smooth direction if m>20
   if m>50: kernel=np.ones(5)/5; d=np.convolve(d,kernel,'same')
   norm=np.linalg.norm(d)
   if norm<1e-14: return None
   d/=norm
   # try steps
   for step in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.6,1.0]:
       trial=np.clip(v - alpha*step*d,0,alpha)
       # also small random noise
       trial=np.clip(trial + rng.normal(0, 0.005*alpha, m),0,alpha)
       sc=fast_score(anti_from_v(trial))
       if sc < fast_score(anti_from_v(v))? We need current score. We can return list? Accept if < global best. In population, compare.
   return None
```

Top-level constants:
```
def next_pow2(x):
    N=1
    while N < x: N <<=1
    return N
```

`convolve_same(a)`: if a.size==1? handle.
```
def convolve_same(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    F=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```

`score_p_single(p)`:
```
s=p.sum()
if s<1e-15: return np.inf
c=convolve_same(p)
if c.size==0: return np.inf
return float(2.0*p.size*np.max(c)/(s*s))
```

`score_p_batch(p)` as above. Need handle p 1D? We'll require 2D. Use `if p.ndim==1: p=p[None,:]`.

`make_weights(c, beta_mult=8.0)`:
```
cmax = float(np.max(c))
mean = float(np.mean(c))
scale = max(cmax - mean, 0.1*cmax, 1e-12)
vals = (c - cmax) * (beta_mult / scale)
vals = np.clip(vals, -30, 0)
w = np.exp(vals)
# zero small
w[w < 1e-6] = 0.0
sw = w.sum()
if sw <= 0:
    w = np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
else: w/=sw
return w
```
Could cmax be 0? returns uniform weights.

`random_anti_v(m, alpha, rng, kind=None)`:
Implement using base beta, etc.
Need m maybe up to 4096. Use functions top-level.
```
def random_anti_v(m, alpha, rng):
    kind = int(rng.integers(0,5))
    if kind==0: # binary
       return rng.choice([0.0, alpha], size=m)
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    if kind==1: # ramp power
       g=0.5+2.0*rng.random()
       v = alpha * (x**g)
       if rng.random()<0.5: v=alpha - v? Actually v in [0,α]; ramp upward or downward: v = alpha * (x**g) or alpha*(1-x)**g
    elif kind==2: # smooth gaussian
       v = rng.normal(size=m)
       # lowpass: apply cosine window? Simpler: convolve with gaussian via FFT
       f=np.fft.rfft(v)
       freq=np.arange(f.size)
       f /= (1.0 + 0.05*freq**1.5)
       w=np.fft.irfft(f,n=m)
       w -= w.mean()
       maxabs=np.max(np.abs(w))
       if maxabs>0: w = w/maxabs * (alpha*0.5)
       v=alpha/2 + w
    elif kind==3: # sine sum
       terms=3
       v=np.zeros(m)
       for _ in range(terms):
          freq=int(rng.integers(1,max(2,m//8)))
          phase=rng.uniform(0,2π)
          amp=alpha*rng.uniform(0.05,0.35)
          v += amp*np.sin(2π*freq*np.arange(m)/m + phase)
       v=alpha/2 + v
    elif kind==4: # random block
       v=np.full(m, alpha/2)
       num=int(rng.integers(1,4))
       for _ in range(num):
          width=int(m*rng.uniform(0.05,0.4))
          start=int(rng.integers(0,m-width))
          v[start:start+width] = (alpha/2)*(1 + rng.uniform(-1,1))
    v=np.clip(v,0.0,alpha)
    return v
```
For m large, loop in sine okay. No closures.

`generate_trial_batch(base, alpha, rng, count)`: as above, uses helpers maybe `random_anti_v` for global.

But be careful: `base` shape (m,), `trials[0]` base. For trial type global, call `random_anti_v(m,alpha,rng)`.

def power_start_vs(n, lambda=1.0):

      p2=x[::-1]**exp
      vs_list.append(anti_ize_p(p2,2*m))
   # exponential

      p=np.exp(-a*x); vs_list.append(anti_ize(p,2*m))
      p=np.exp(a*x); vs_list.append(...)
   # random smooths fill

`population_anti_search(n, deadline, rng, initial_vs)`:
Implementation details:
```
def population_anti_search(n, deadline, rng, initial_vs=None):
    m=n//2
    alpha=2.0/n
    max_pop=10 if n<=1024 else 6
    vs_list=[]
    if initial_vs:
       for v in initial_vs[:max_pop]:
          v=np.clip(np.asarray(v),0,alpha)
          if v.size==m: vs_list.append(v)
    while len(vs_list)<max_pop:
       vs_list.append(random_anti_v(m,alpha,rng))
    vs=np.vstack([v[:m] for v in vs_list])
    _, scores = score_anti_batch(vs)
    # replace infs
    bad=~np.isfinite(scores)
    if bad.any(): ...
    best_i=np.argmin(scores)
    best_v=vs[best_i].copy(); best_s=scores[best_i]
    no_improve=0
    last_grad=0.0
    last_log=0.0
    round_i=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # maybe terminate if very close? still improve
        # Generate trials
        count = 8 if n<=512 else 4
        base = vs[round_i % max_pop]
        trials = generate_trial_batch(base, alpha, rng, count, best_v)
        # Add gradient trials for best occasionally
        if time.monotonic() - last_grad > 2.0:
            gtrials, sc_best_cached = anti_gradient_trials(best_v, alpha, rng)
            # score gtrials with batch maybe combine
            n_trials = trials.shape[0] + len(gtrials)
            all_trials = np.vstack([trials, np.vstack(gtrials) if len(gtrials)>1 else gtrials[0][None]])
            ... score
            last_grad = now
        else: only trials
        _, tscores = score_anti_batch(all_trials)
        min_idx = argmin(tscores)
        trial = all_trials[min_idx]; ts=tscores[min_idx]
        # Accept with temperature comparing to base score? Use current pop score
        max_accept = scores[base_idx] + 0.02*(1.0 + no_improve*0.0001)
        if ts < scores[base_idx] or (ts < max_accept and score < best_s + 0.1? no):
           vs[base_idx]=trial; scores[base_idx]=ts
        if ts < best_s:
           best_v=trial.copy(); best_s=ts; no_improve=0; 
           # put best in a random pop
           vs[base_idx]=best_v.copy(); scores[base_idx]=best_s
        else:
           no_improve += 1
        # Restart worst if no_improve large
        if no_improve > 500:
           worst = int(np.argmax(scores))
           vs[worst] = random_anti_v(m,alpha,rng)
           _, sc = score_anti_batch(vs[worst][None,:])
           scores[worst]=sc[0]
           if sc[0] < best_s: update
           no_improve=0
        round_i +=1
        if time - last_log > 10: print
    return best_v, best_s
```
Need handle `all_trials` when gradient trials empty.

But note: If we accept a worse trial into population based on temperature, score can increase; later if gradient uses best okay. Need not too high temperature to avoid lost. Set accept if `ts < scores[base_idx] + 0.03 * max(1.0, best_s)` but only if `ts < best_s + 0.5`? Maybe simpler accept improvements only, and use random restarts for exploration. Pure improvements with random restarts may be enough and simpler. Let's avoid temperature complexity.

- For each selected base, generate trials. Replace base with best trial if better than base. Update global best.
- If no global improvement for 1000 iterations, reset worst with random (or insert random with better than worst? Even if random worse, it increases population diversity but scores array worsens? We can reset worst regardless to new random; if new random worse than worst? worst is max so new likely better or not; but diversity). This periodic restart helps.
- Gradient trials for best likely improve.
This is deterministic-ish.

```
def upsample_v(best_v, new_n):
    old_n=2*best_v.size
    p=anti_from_v(best_v)
    if old_n == new_n: return best_v
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,old_n)
    x_new=np.linspace(0,1,new_n)
    p_new=np.interp(x_new,x_old,p)
    v=anti_ize_p(p_new, new_n)
    return v
```

```
def smooth_anti_fun(v, n, beta, mode):
    alpha=2.0/n
    m=n//2
    c=convolve_same(anti_from_v(v))
    cmax=c.max()
    scale=max(cmax-np.mean(c),0.1*cmax,1e-12)
    vals=(c-cmax)*(beta/scale)
    vals=np.clip(vals,-50,0)
    exps=np.exp(vals)
    sw=exps.sum()
    if sw<=0: sw=1; weights = onehot
    else: weights=exps/sw
    value = 2.0*n*(cmax + np.log(sw))/beta
    if mode:
       g=gradient_from_weights(weights, anti_from_v(v))
       dv = g[:m]-g[m:][::-1]
       return value, (2.0*n/beta)*dv
    return value
```

def general_population_search(n, deadline, rng, initial_ps):
   P=6
   ps=[]
   if initial_ps: add normalized
   while len<P: ps.append(random_general_p(n,rng))
   ps=np.vstack(ps)
   _, scores=score_p_batch(ps)
   best=...
   no_imp=0; round_i=0
   while time<deadline:
      base_idx=round_i%P; base=ps[base_idx]
      count = 8 if n<=512 else 4
      trials=generate_general_trials(base,n,rng,count,best_p=ps[best_idx])
      # gradient trials for best occasionally
      if time-last_grad>2: gtrials,sc=general_gradient_trials(base or best?); combine
      _, ts=score_p_batch(trials)

      no_imp... restart worst with random general
      round_i++
   return best_p,best_s

Detailed code snippets:

```
def normalize_batch(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    if p.ndim == 1:
        s = p.sum()
        if s <= 1e-300: return np.ones_like(p)/p.size
        return p/s
    sums = p.sum(axis=1)
    bad = sums <= 1e-300
    if np.any(bad):
        p = p.copy()
        p[bad] = 1.0/p.shape[1]
        sums[bad] = 1.0
    return p / sums[:, None]
```
For anti, not use.

`general_random_p(n,rng)`:
```
def random_general_p(n, rng):
   kind=int(rng.integers(0,4))
   if kind==0:
      conc=rng.choice([0.1,0.5,1.0,3.0,10.0])
      p=rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, conc))
   elif kind==1:
      x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
      e=rng.uniform(0.25,3.0)
      flip=rng.random()<0.5
      if flip: x=1-x
      p=x**e
   elif kind==2:
      p=np.abs(rng.normal(size=n))
      # smooth
      f=np.fft.rfft(p)
      freqs=np.arange(f.size)
      f /= (1.0 + 0.2*freqs)
      p=np.fft.irfft(f,n=n)
   else:
      p=np.ones(n)
      for _ in range(rng.integers(1,4)):
         w=max(1,int(n*rng.uniform(0.05,0.3)))
         s=int(rng.integers(0,n-w))
         p[s:s+w] *= rng.uniform(0.1,3.0)
   p=np.maximum(p,0)
   s=p.sum()
   if s<=0: p=np.ones(n)
   return p/s
```
No lambdas.

`generate_general_trials(base,n,best_p, rng, count)`:
```
def generate_general_trials(base, n, best_p, rng, count):
    B=count+1
    trials=np.empty((B,n))
    trials[0]=base
    uniform=np.full(n,1.0/n)
    for i in range(1,B):
       t=base.copy()
       typ=int(rng.integers(0,5))
       if typ==0:
          width=max(1,int(n*rng.uniform(0.02,0.25)))
          st=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
          t[st:st+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.35))
       elif typ==1:
          sigma = 0.05 / n
          t += rng.normal(0.0,sigma,n)
       elif typ==2:
          lam=rng.uniform(0.1,0.5)
          t=(1-lam)*base + lam*uniform + rng.normal(0.0,0.02/n,n)
       elif typ==3:
          lam=rng.uniform(0.2,0.8)
          t=(1-lam)*base + lam*best_p + rng.normal(0.0,0.02/n,n)
       else:
          t=random_general_p(n,rng)
       if not np.isfinite(t).all(): t=random_general_p(n,rng)
       trials[i]=np.maximum(t,0.0)
    return normalize_batch(trials)
```
Need `reversed`? okay.

`general_gradient_trials(base, rng)`:
- base is best p maybe. Score current? Return trials only; population will score them.
```
def general_gradient_trials(p, rng):
   n=p.size
   c=convolve_same(p)
   w=make_weights(c)
   g=gradient_from_weights(w,p)
   d=g - np.mean(g)
   if n>100:
      d=np.convolve(d, np.ones(5)/5.0, mode='same')
   norm=np.linalg.norm(d)
   steps=[0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4]
   trials=[]
   if norm <= 1e-15:
      return np.tile(p, (len(steps),1)), float(score_p_single(p))
   d=d/norm
   for st in steps:
      t=p - st*d
      t=np.maximum(t,0.0)
      s=t.sum()
      if s<=0: t=p.copy()
      else: t=t/s
      if not finite: t=p
      trials.append(t)
   return np.vstack(trials), score_p_single(p)
```
Note `st` here is step fraction, not exponent. For n large, d unit, subtract 0.4 maybe changes some p by 0.4 > p avg 0.002, many zeros; normalize. Is that too aggressive? `generate_trial_batch` must handle global random and best blend.
```
def generate_trial_batch(base, alpha, rng, count, best_v):
    m=base.size
    B=count+1
    trials=np.empty((B,m))
    trials[0]=base
    uniform=np.full(m,0.5*alpha)
    for i in range(1,B):
       t=base.copy()
       typ=int(rng.integers(0,5))
       if typ==0:
           sigma=alpha*rng.uniform(0.02,0.12)
           t += rng.normal(0.0,sigma,m)
       elif typ==1:
           width=max(1, int(m*rng.uniform(0.02,0.2)))
           st=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,m-width+1)))
           t[st:st+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.3))
       elif typ==2:
           lam=rng.uniform(0.1,0.4)
           t=(1-lam)*base + lam*uniform + rng.normal(0.0,0.01*alpha,m)
       elif typ==3:
           lam=rng.uniform(0.2,0.8)
           t=(1-lam)*base + lam*best_v + rng.normal(0.0,0.01*alpha,m)
       else:
           t=random_anti_v(m,alpha,rng)
       np.clip(t,0.0,alpha,out=t)
       trials[i]=t
    return trials
```
Good.

def anti_gradient_trials(v, alpha, rng):
    m=v.size; n=2*m
    p=anti_from_v(v)
    c=convolve_same(p)
    w=make_weights(c)
    g=gradient_from_weights(w,p)
    d=g[:m] - g[m:][::-1]
    if m>30:
       d=np.convolve(d, np.ones(5)/5.0, 'same')
    norm=np.linalg.norm(d)
    scale_values=[0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8]
    trials=[]
    if norm<=1e-15:
       for _ in scale_values: trials.append(v.copy())
       return np.vstack(trials), score_p_single(p)
    d=d/norm
    for sv in scale_values:
       t=np.clip(v - alpha*sv*d,0.0,alpha)
       # If no movement due to one-sided, try smaller or random?
       if np.allclose(t,v):
           t=np.clip(v + rng.normal(0.0,0.02*alpha,m),0.0,alpha)
       trials.append(t)
    return np.vstack(trials), score_p_single(p)
```

def smooth_anti_scalar(v, beta_mult):
    v=np.asarray(v,float)
    m=v.size; n=2*m; alpha=2.0/n
    v=np.clip(v,0.0,alpha)
    p=anti_from_v(v)
    c=convolve_same(p)
    cmax=float(np.max(c))
    mean=float(np.mean(c))
    scale=max(cmax-mean, 0.1*cmax, 1e-12)
    beta=beta_mult/scale
    vals=np.clip((c-cmax)*beta, -50.0, 0.0)
    exps=np.exp(vals)
    sw=float(np.sum(exps))
    if sw<=0: sw=1.0
    return float(2.0*n*(cmax + np.log(sw))/beta)

def smooth_anti_grad(v, beta_mult):
    ... compute weights; p; g=gradient_from_weights(weights,p); dv=g[:m]-g[m:][::-1]; return value, (2.0*n/beta)*dv
```

`project_simplex` implementation with correction:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    v=np.asarray(v,float)
    n=v.size
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css=np.cumsum(u)
    cond=u - (css-1.0)/np.arange(1,n+1)
    rho=np.nonzero(cond>0)[0]
    if rho.size==0:
       lam=css[-1]/n
    else:
       lam=css[rho[-1]]/(rho[-1]+1)
    return np.maximum(v-lam,0.0)
```
Check formula: standard: ρ largest k such that u_k - (sum_{i≤k}u_i -1)/k >0. Here cond >0. If none, λ=mean? For all negative? return 0? In standard, if no k, λ=(sum u)/n? But if v all ≤0, sum maybe<1, projection to simplex? Actually projection onto {x≥0,sum1}; if all v negative, should distribute evenly? Formula: if no ρ, λ = (sum u)/n? For v all negative, sum u could be negative, λ negative, v-λ positive? But sum not1? Standard sets λ = max(u) maybe? Let's use robust:
```
rho = np.nonzero(cond > 0)[0]
if len(rho)==0:
   # assign uniform
   return np.full_like(v, 1.0/n)
lam = (css[rho[-1]] - 1.0) / (rho[-1] + 1.0)
y=np.maximum(v-lam,0)
s=y.sum()
if s<=0: return uniform
return y/s  # guard
```
Adding final normalize fixes any error. Good.

def interpolate_sequence(p,n):
   p=np.asarray(p,float); p=np.clip(p,0,None); s=p.sum(); if s=0 return np.full(n,1/n)
   p=p/s
   if p.size==n: return p.copy()
   x_old=np.linspace(0,1,p.size); x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   q=np.interp(x_new,x_old,p)
   return normalize_batch(q)[0]

```
def anti_ize_p(p,n):
   m=n//2
   alpha=2.0/n
   p=np.asarray(p,float).copy()
   if p.size != n:
      p=interpolate_sequence(p,n)
   p=np.clip(p,0,None)
   s=p.sum()
   if s<=0: return np.full(m,0.5*alpha)
   p=p/s
   left=p[:m]
   right=p[m:][::-1]
   v=0.5*alpha + 0.5*(left-right)
   # The difference may exceed alpha, clip. But to maximize amplitude? If left-right large, clip loses. Could scale to fit before adding:
   diff=left-right
   maxabs=np.max(np.abs(diff))
   # pair sum constraint mean α/2; max allowed diff α. raw diff may be >α. Scale down if maxabs>α:
   if maxabs > alpha:
       diff *= (alpha/maxabs)
   v=0.5*alpha + 0.5*diff
   return np.clip(v,0.0,alpha)
```
This preserves relative. Good.

Top-level `sine_objective(coeffs, n)`:
```
def sine_coeff_vector(coeffs, n):
   m? alpha=2/n
   i=np.arange(n)
   p=np.full(n,0.5*alpha)
   amp=0.5*alpha
   for j in range(len(coeffs)):
      b=float(coeffs[j])
      if abs(b)>1e-8: p += amp * b * np.sin(2*np.pi*(j+1)*i/n)
   v=anti_ize_p(p,n)
   return v
```
`anti_ize_p` will scale difference to fit α, but if raw p out [0,α], clipping in v? In anti_ize_p, it normalizes p sum to1 (raw sum1), computes diff = left-right, if maxabs>α scale to α, v=α/2+0.5*diff. This is nonlinear but okay.
Actually if raw p can be negative, diff scale may represent shape; clipping v to [0,α] after. Good.

def sine_anti_search(n, deadline, rng):
   if scipy_opt is None: return None,None
   K=6 if n>1024 else 8
   start = time.monotonic()
   # Maybe maximize time but set maxiter based on remaining
   rem = deadline - start
   maxiter = int(max(30, min(200, rem // 0.5))) # ~ each iter 0.1s?
   popsize=10
   try:
      res= scipy_opt.differential_evolution(sine_objective, [(-1,1)]*K, args=(n,), maxiter=maxiter, tol=1e-5, popsize=popsize, seed=int(rng.integers(2**31-1)), polish=False, initial='sohal'?)
   except: return None,None
   if res and res.x is not None:
      v=sine_coeff_vector(res.x,n)
      s=fast_score(anti_from_v(v))
      return v,s
```

```
def tiny_binary_anti_search(deadline):
   best_p=None; best=inf
   for n in [8,12,16,20,24]:
      m=n//2
      cnt=1<<m
      if cnt > 200000: break
      alpha=2/n
      for mask in range(cnt):
         bits=np.empty(n)
         For i in range(m): bits[i]= (mask>>i)&1
         # complement reverse: bits[n-1-i] = 1-bits[i]
         # Do loop for small m
         for i in range(m): bits[n-1-i]=1-bits[i]
         b=bits
         c=np.convolve(b,b) # returns int arrays? numpy int maybe; fine
         rmax=int(c.max())
         score=8.0*rmax/n
         if score<best: store p = alpha*bits
      if time > deadline break
   return best_p,best
```

       # maybe reduce if best_s < TARGET? Continue smaller?

```python
"""
Search for low 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/sum(a)^2 nonnegative sequences.

The routine combines several complementary methods:

1. A few exhaustive / lattice searches for very short anti-palindromic
   binary sequences.  These are cheap and can sometimes already hit the
   target.

2. A vectorized population/refinement search for anti-palindromic
   sequences p_i + p_{n-1-i} = 2/n.  These automatically cancel the
   central convolution lag and avoid a large part of the usual peak.

3. Low-dimensional global searches over anti-palindromic sine + ramp
   basis coefficients using differential evolution, when SciPy is
   available.

4. A short general non-anti search with stochastic renormalization and
   subgradient/gradient-like moves.

All fitness evaluations used inside the search are O(n log n) FFT
convolutions.  The final answer is validated with the official
evaluate_sequence() when the length is not too large.
"""

import time
import math
import numpy as np

try:
    import scipy.optimize as scipy_opt
except Exception:
    scipy_opt = None


TARGET_SCORE = 1.5027


def next_pow2(x):
    N = 1
    while N < int(x):
        N <<= 1
    return N


def convolve_same(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    N = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, n=N)
    c = c[:2 * n - 1]
    c[~np.isfinite(c)] = 0.0
    return c


def score_p_single(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.inf
    if np.any(p < -1e-12) or not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
        p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
        s = float(np.sum(p))
        if s <= 1e-300:
            return np.inf
        p = p / s
    c = convolve_same(p)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    cm = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cm) or cm < 0:
        cm = max(cm, 0.0)
    val = 2.0 * n * cm / (s * s)
    return float(val) if np.isfinite(val) else np.inf


def score_p_batch(p):
    p = np.array(p, dtype=np.float64, copy=False)
    if p.ndim == 1:
        p = p[None, :]
    if p.shape[0] == 0:
        return np.zeros(0), np.zeros(0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    n = p.shape[1]
    N = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, N, axis=1)
    C = np.fft.irfft(F * F, n=N, axis=1)
    C = C[:, :2 * n - 1]
    cmax = np.max(C, axis=1)
    sums = np.sum(p, axis=1)
    bad = (sums <= 1e-300) | (~np.isfinite(sums))
    if np.any(bad):
        p_fix = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
        p_fix[bad] = 1.0 / n
        cmax_fix = np.zeros(p.shape[0])
        for idx in np.where(bad)[0]:
            cmax_fix[idx] = np.max(convolve_same(p_fix[idx]))
        cmax = np.where(bad, cmax_fix, cmax)
        sums = np.where(bad, 1.0, sums)
    with np.errstate(divide="ignore", invalid="ignore"):
        scores = 2.0 * n * cmax / (sums * sums)
    scores[~np.isfinite(scores)] = np.inf
    return cmax, scores.astype(np.float64)


def score_anti_batch(vs):
    vs = np.asarray(vs, dtype=np.float64)
    if vs.ndim == 1:
        vs = vs[None, :]
    m = vs.shape[1]
    n = 2 * m
    alpha = 2.0 / n
    p = np.empty((vs.shape[0], n), dtype=np.float64)
    p[:, :m] = vs
    p[:, m:] = alpha - np.flip(vs, axis=1)
    return score_p_batch(p)


def normalize_batch(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim == 1:
        p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
        s = float(np.sum(p))
        if s <= 1e-300:
            return np.ones_like(p) / max(1, p.size)
        return p / s
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    sums = np.sum(p, axis=1)
    bad = (sums <= 1e-300) | (~np.isfinite(sums))
    if np.any(bad):
        p = p.copy()
        p[bad] = 1.0 / p.shape[1]
        sums = np.where(bad, 1.0, sums)
    out = p / sums[:, None]
    out[~np.isfinite(out)] = 1.0 / p.shape[1]
    return out


def project_simplex(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0)
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u - (css - 1.0) / idx
    rho = np.nonzero(cond > 0.0)[0]
    if rho.size == 0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    lam = (css[rho[-1]] - 1.0) / (rho[-1] + 1.0)
    y = np.maximum(v - lam, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(y))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    return y / s


def anti_from_v(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    m = v.size
    n = 2 * m
    alpha = 2.0 / n
    p = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    p[:m] = v
    p[m:] = alpha - v[::-1]
    p[~np.isfinite(p)] = 0.0
    return np.clip(p, 0.0, alpha)


def interpolate_sequence(p, n):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if p.size == 0 or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(max(1, int(n)), 1.0 / max(1, int(n)), dtype=np.float64)
    p = p / s
    n = int(n)
    if p.size == n:
        return p.copy()
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, p.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    q = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
    return normalize_batch(q)


def anti_ize_p(p, n):
    n = int(n)
    if n % 2 == 1:
        n -= 1
    if n < 2:
        return np.zeros(0)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size != n:
        p = interpolate_sequence(p, n)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(n // 2, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    p = p / s
    m = n // 2
    alpha = 2.0 / n
    left = p[:m]
    right = p[m:][::-1]
    diff = left - right
    maxabs = float(np.max(np.abs(diff))) if diff.size else 0.0
    if maxabs > alpha:
        diff = diff * (alpha / maxabs)
    v = 0.5 * alpha + 0.5 * diff
    return np.clip(v, 0.0, alpha)


def upsample_v_from_p(p, new_n):
    p2 = interpolate_sequence(p, new_n)
    return anti_ize_p(p2, new_n)


def make_weights(c, beta_mult=10.0):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    mean = float(np.mean(c))
    scale = max(cmax - mean, 0.1 * max(cmax, 1e-12), 1e-12)
    vals = (c - cmax) * (float(beta_mult) / scale)
    vals = np.clip(vals, -32.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(vals)
    w[w < 1e-8] = 0.0
    sw = float(np.sum(w))
    if sw <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(sw):
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= sw
    return w


def gradient_from_weights(w, p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0)
    r = p[::-1]
    N = next_pow2(3 * n + 2)
    Fw = np.fft.rfft(np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64), N)
    Fp = np.fft.rfft(r, N)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(Fw * Fp, n=N)
    start = n - 1
    end = start + n
    if conv.size < end:
        pad = np.zeros(end - conv.size, dtype=np.float64)
        conv = np.concatenate([conv, pad])
    g = 2.0 * conv[start:end]
    g[~np.isfinite(g)] = 0.0
    return g


def random_general_p(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    kind = int(rng.integers(0, 5))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    if kind == 0:
        conc = float(rng.choice([0.1, 0.3, 1.0, 3.0, 10.0]))
        p = rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, conc))
    elif kind == 1:
        e = float(rng.uniform(0.25, 3.0))
        xx = x if float(rng.random()) < 0.5 else 1.0 - x
        p = xx ** e
    elif kind == 2:
        p = np.abs(rng.normal(size=n))
        F = np.fft.rfft(p)
        freq = np.arange(F.size, dtype=np.float64)
        F = F / (1.0 + 0.25 * freq)
        p = np.fft.irfft(F, n=n)
        p = np.abs(p)
    elif kind == 3:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for _ in range(int(rng.integers(1, 4))):
            w = max(1, int(n * float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.35))))
            st = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - w + 1)))
            p[st:st + w] *= float(rng.uniform(0.05, 3.0))
    else:
        p = x ** float(rng.uniform(0.5, 2.0))
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    return p / s


def random_anti_v(m, alpha, rng):
    m = int(m)
    kind = int(rng.integers(0, 6))
    ix = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    if kind == 0:
        return rng.choice([0.0, float(alpha)], size=m).astype(np.float64)
    if kind == 1:
        e = float(rng.uniform(0.4, 2.8))
        v = alpha * (ix ** e)
        if float(rng.random()) < 0.5:
            v = alpha - v
        return np.clip(v, 0.0, alpha)
    if kind == 2:
        g = rng.normal(size=m)
        F = np.fft.rfft(g)
        freq = np.arange(F.size, dtype=np.float64)
        F = F / (1.0 + 0.04 * freq ** 1.25)
        w = np.fft.irfft(F, n=m)
        w -= float(np.mean(w))
        amax = float(np.max(np.abs(w))) if w.size else 0.0
        if amax > 1e-15:
            w = w / amax * (0.5 * alpha)
        return np.clip(0.5 * alpha + w, 0.0, alpha)
    if kind == 3:
        v = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
        jx = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)
        for _ in range(int(rng.integers(2, 5))):
            freq = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, m // 6 + 1)))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi))
            amp = float(alpha * rng.uniform(0.05, 0.35))
            v += amp * np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * freq * jx / max(1, m) + phase)
        return np.clip(0.5 * alpha + v, 0.0, alpha)
    if kind == 4:
        v = np.full(m, 0.5 * alpha, dtype=np.float64)
        for _ in range(int(rng.integers(1, 4))):
            w = max(1, int(m * float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.45))))
            st = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, m - w + 1)))
            v[st:st + w] = 0.5 * alpha * float(rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0))
        return np.clip(v, 0.0, alpha)
    # Gentle linear/square anti-template.
    t = float(rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0))
    base = (1.0 - math.cosh(t)) * 0.5
    return np.clip(0.5 * alpha * (1.0 + t * (2.0 * ix - 1.0) + 0.3 * math.sin(2.0 * math.pi * ix)), 0.0, alpha)


def generate_trial_batch(base, alpha, rng, count, best_v):
    m = int(base.size)
    B = int(count) + 1
    trials = np.empty((B, m), dtype=np.float64)
    trials[0] = base
    uniform = np.full(m, 0.5 * float(alpha), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(1, B):
        t = base.copy()
        typ = int(rng.integers(0, 6))
        if typ == 0:
            sigma = float(alpha * rng.uniform(0.02, 0.13))
            t += rng.normal(0.0, sigma, m)
        elif typ == 1:
            w = max(1, int(m * float(rng.uniform(0.02, 0.22))))
            st = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, m - w + 1)))
            t[st:st + w] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.35)))
        elif typ == 2:
            lam = float(rng.uniform(0.1, 0.45))
            t = (1.0 - lam) * base + lam * uniform
            t += rng.normal(0.0, 0.01 * float(alpha), m)
        elif typ == 3:
            lam = float(rng.uniform(0.25, 0.85))
            t = (1.0 - lam) * base + lam * best_v
            t += rng.normal(0.0, 0.01 * float(alpha), m)
        elif typ == 4:
            # Local phase perturbation.
            w = max(1, int(m * float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.3))))
            st = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, m - w + 1)))
            amp = float(alpha * rng.uniform(0.05, 0.5))
            jx = np.arange(st, st + w, dtype=np.float64)
            t[st:st + w] += amp * np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * float(rng.integers(1, max(2, m // 8 + 1))) * jx / max(1, m) + float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi)))
        else:
            t = random_anti_v(m, alpha, rng)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(t)):
            t = random_anti_v(m, alpha, rng)
        trials[i] = np.clip(t, 0.0, alpha)
    return trials


def anti_gradient_trials(v, alpha, rng):
    m = int(v.size)
    n = 2 * m
    p = anti_from_v(v)
    c = convolve_same(p)
    w = make_weights(c, beta_mult=10.0)
    g = gradient_from_weights(w, p)
    d = g[:m] - g[m:][::-1]
    if m > 40:
        kernel = np.ones(5) / 5.0
        d = np.convolve(d, kernel, mode="same")
    norm = float(np.linalg.norm(d))
    steps = [0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4, 0.8]
    if norm <= 1e-15:
        return np.tile(v, (len(steps), 1)), score_p_single(p)
    d = d / norm
    trials = []
    for st in steps:
        t = v - float(alpha) * st * d
        if float(np.linalg.norm(t - v)) <= 1e-14:
            t = v + rng.normal(0.0, 0.02 * float(alpha), m)
        trials.append(np.clip(t, 0.0, alpha))
    return np.vstack(trials), score_p_single(p)


def power_start_vs(n):
    m = int(n) // 2
    if m <= 0:
        return []
    n2 = 2 * m
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n2)
    vs = []
    for e in (0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.25, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0):
        v = anti_ize_p(x ** e, n2)
        if v.size == m:
            vs.append(v)
        v = anti_ize_p((x[::-1]) ** e, n2)
        if v.size == m:
            vs.append(v)
    for a in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        v = anti_ize_p(np.exp(a * x), n2)
        if v.size == m:
            vs.append(v)
        v = anti_ize_p(np.exp(-a * x), n2)
        if v.size == m:
            vs.append(v)
    return vs[:18]


def general_population_search(n, deadline, rng, initial_ps=None):
    n = int(n)
    max_pop = 5 if n <= 512 else 3
    plist = []
    if initial_ps is not None:
        for p in initial_ps:
            p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
            if p.size == n and np.all(np.isfinite(p)) and np.sum(np.maximum(p, 0.0)) > 1e-12:
                plist.append(normalize_batch(p))
    while len(plist) < max_pop:
        plist.append(random_general_p(n, rng))
    P = np.vstack(plist)[:max_pop]
    _, scores = score_p_batch(P)
    bad = ~np.isfinite(scores)
    for idx in np.where(bad)[0]:
        P[idx] = random_general_p(n, rng)
        _, sc = score_p_batch(P[idx][None, :])
        scores[idx] = sc[0]
    best_i = int(np.argmin(scores))
    best_p = P[best_i].copy()
    best_s = float(scores[best_i])
    no_improve = 0
    round_i = 0
    last_grad = 0.0
    last_log = 0.0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        base_idx = round_i % P
        base = P[base_idx].copy()
        count = 10 if n <= 512 else 5
        trials = generate_general_trials(base, n, best_p, rng, count)
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now - last_grad > 2.5:
            gtrials, _ = general_gradient_trials(best_p, rng)
            trials = np.vstack([trials, gtrials])
            last_grad = now
        _, tscores = score_p_batch(trials)
        min_i = int(np.argmin(tscores))
        ts = float(tscores[min_i])
        if ts < scores[base_idx]:
            P[base_idx] = trials[min_i]
            scores[base_idx] = ts
        if ts < best_s:
            best_p = trials[min_i].copy()
            best_s = ts
            no_improve = 0
            P[base_idx] = best_p.copy()
            scores[base_idx] = best_s
        else:
            no_improve += 1
        if no_improve > 700:
            worst = int(np.argmax(scores))
            P[worst] = random_general_p(n, rng)
            _, sc = score_p_batch(P[worst][None, :])
            scores[worst] = sc[0]
            if sc[0] < best_s:
                best_p = P[worst].copy()
                best_s = float(sc[0])
            no_improve = 0
        round_i += 1
        if now - last_log > 15.0:
            print("general n=%d best=%.6f" % (n, best_s), flush=True)
            last_log = now
    return best_p, best_s


def generate_general_trials(base, n, best_p, rng, count):
    B = int(count) + 1
    T = np.empty((B, n), dtype=np.float64)
    T[0] = base
    uniform = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(1, B):
        t = base.copy()
        typ = int(rng.integers(0, 5))
        if typ == 0:
            w = max(1, int(n * float(rng.uniform(0.02, 0.25))))
            st = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - w + 1)))
            t[st:st + w] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.35)))
        elif typ == 1:
            sigma = 0.05 / float(n)
            t += rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
        elif typ == 2:
            lam = float(rng.uniform(0.1, 0.45))
            t = (1.0 - lam) * base + lam * uniform
            t += rng.normal(0.0, 0.02 / float(n), n)
        elif typ == 3:
            lam = float(rng.uniform(0.25, 0.85))
            t = (1.0 - lam) * base + lam * best_p
            t += rng.normal(0.0, 0.02 / float(n), n)
        else:
            t = random_general_p(n, rng)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(t)):
            t = random_general_p(n, rng)
        T[i] = np.maximum(t, 0.0)
    return normalize_batch(T)


def general_gradient_trials(p, rng):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    c = convolve_same(p)
    w = make_weights(c, beta_mult=10.0)
    g = gradient_from_weights(w, p)
    d = g - float(np.mean(g))
    if n > 80:
        d = np.convolve(d, np.ones(5) / 5.0, mode="same")
    norm = float(np.linalg.norm(d))
    scales = [0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 8.0]
    if norm <= 1e-15:
        return np.tile(p, (len(scales), 1)), score_p_single(p)
    d = d / norm
    trials = []
    for sc in scales:
        t = project_simplex(p - (sc / float(n)) * d)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(t)):
            t = p.copy()
        trials.append(t)
    return np.vstack(trials), score_p_single(p)


def population_anti_search(n, deadline, rng, initial_vs=None):
    m = int(n) // 2
    if m <= 0:
        return None, np.inf
    alpha = 2.0 / float(n)
    max_pop = 12 if n <= 512 else 6
    vs_list = []
    if initial_vs is not None:
        for v in initial_vs:
            v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
            if v.size == m and np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
                vs_list.append(np.clip(v, 0.0, alpha))
    while len(vs_list) < max_pop:
        vs_list.append(random_anti_v(m, alpha, rng))
    VS = np.vstack(vs_list[:max_pop])
    _, scores = score_anti_batch(VS)
    bad = ~np.isfinite(scores)
    for idx in np.where(bad)[0]:
        VS[idx] = random_anti_v(m, alpha, rng)
        _, sc = score_anti_batch(VS[idx][None, :])
        scores[idx] = sc[0]
    best_i = int(np.argmin(scores))
    best_v = VS[best_i].copy()
    best_s = float(scores[best_i])
    no_improve = 0
    round_i = 0
    last_grad = 0.0
    last_log = 0.0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        base_idx = round_i % VS
        base = VS[base_idx].copy()
        count = 18 if n <= 256 else (10 if n <= 1024 else 5)
        trials = generate_trial_batch(base, alpha, rng, count, best_v)
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now - last_grad > 2.0:
            gtrials, _ = anti_gradient_trials(best_v, alpha, rng)
            trials = np.vstack([trials, gtrials])
            last_grad = now
        _, tscores = score_anti_batch(trials)
        min_i = int(np.argmin(tscores))
        ts = float(tscores[min_i])
        if ts < scores[base_idx]:
            VS[base_idx] = trials[min_i]
            scores[base_idx] = ts
        if ts < best_s:
            best_v = trials[min_i].copy()
            best_s = ts
            no_improve = 0
            VS[base_idx] = best_v.copy()
            scores[base_idx] = best_s
        else:
            no_improve += 1
        if no_improve > 900:
            worst = int(np.argmax(scores))
            VS[worst] = random_anti_v(m, alpha, rng)
            _, sc = score_anti_batch(VS[worst][None, :])
            scores[worst] = sc[0]
            if sc[0] < best_s:
                best_v = VS[worst].copy()
                best_s = float(sc[0])
            no_improve = 0
        round_i += 1
        if now - last_log > 15.0:
            print("anti n=%d best=%.6f" % (n, best_s), flush=True)
            last_log = now
    return best_v, best_s


def sine_coeff_vector(coeffs, n):
    coeffs = np.asarray(coeffs, dtype=np.float64)
    m = int(n) // 2
    if m <= 0:
        return None
    p = anti_ize_p(np.ones(m) * 0.5, 2 * m)  # placeholder for alpha defensive
    # Construct raw anti-like sequence.
    p = np.full(m, 0.5 * (2.0 / float(n)), dtype=np.float64)
    amp = 0.5 * (2.0 / float(n))
    c0 = float(np.clip(coeffs[0], -1.0, 1.0)) if coeffs.size > 0 else 0.0
    p += amp * c0 * (2.0 * np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, m - 1) - 1.0)
    for j in range(1, min(coeffs.size, 8)):
        b = float(np.clip(coeffs[j], -1.0, 1.0))
        if abs(b) < 1e-12:
            continue
        p += amp * b * np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * j * np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, m))
    # Use the anti-izer to make a valid anti vector, also clipping/scaling.
    return np.clip(p, 0.0, 2.0 / float(n))


def sine_objective(coeffs, n):
    try:
        v = sine_coeff_vector(coeffs, n)
        if v is None or v.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
            return 1.0e9
        p = anti_from_v(v)
        s = score_p_single(p)
        return s if np.isfinite(s) else 1.0e9
    except Exception:
        return 1.0e9


def sine_anti_search(n, deadline, rng):
    if scipy_opt is None:
        return None, np.inf
    m = int(n) // 2
    if m <= 0:
        return None, np.inf
    K = 5 if n > 1024 else 7
    budget = max(1.0, float(deadline - time.monotonic()))
    maxiter = int(max(15, min(70, budget / 2.0)))
    bounds = [(-1.0, 1.0)] * K
    try:
        res = scipy_opt.differential_evolution(
            sine_objective,
            bounds,
            args=(n,),
            maxiter=maxiter,
            popsize=10,
            tol=1e-5,
            seed=int(rng.integers(0, 2**31 - 1)),
            polish=False,
            updating="immediate",
        )
    except Exception:
        return None, np.inf
    if res is None or not hasattr(res, "x") or res.x is None:
        return None, np.inf
    v = sine_coeff_vector(res.x, n)
    if v is None or v.size != m:
        return None, np.inf
    s = score_p_single(anti_from_v(v))
    if not np.isfinite(s):
        return None, np.inf
    return v, float(s)


def smooth_anti_scalar(v, beta_mult):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    m = v.size
    n = 2 * m
    alpha = 2.0 / float(n)
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, alpha)
    p = anti_from_v(v)
    c = convolve_same(p)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    mean = float(np.mean(c))
    scale = max(cmax - mean, 0.1 * max(cmax, 1e-12), 1e-12)
    beta = float(beta_mult) / scale
    vals = np.clip((c - cmax) * beta, -50.0, 0.0)
    sw = float(np.sum(np.exp(vals)))
    if sw <= 1e-300:
        return 1.0e9
    return float(2.0 * n * (cmax + math.log(sw)) / beta)


def smooth_anti_grad(v, beta_mult):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    m = v.size
    n = 2 * m
    alpha = 2.0 / float(n)
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, alpha)
    p = anti_from_v(v)
    c = convolve_same(p)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    mean = float(np.mean(c))
    scale = max(cmax - mean, 0.1 * max(cmax, 1e-12), 1e-12)
    beta = float(beta_mult) / scale
    vals = np.clip((c - cmax) * beta, -50.0, 0.0)
    exps = np.exp(vals)
    sw = float(np.sum(exps))
    if sw <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w = exps / sw
    g = gradient_from_weights(w, p)
    gv = g[:m] - g[m:][::-1]
    gv = np.zeros(m) if gv.size == 0 else gv
    gv[~np.isfinite(gv)] = 0.0
    value = float(2.0 * n * (cmax + math.log(max(sw, 1.0e-300))) / beta)
    return value, (2.0 * n / beta) * gv


def polish_anti(n, v0, deadline, rng):
    if scipy_opt is None or v0 is None:
        return v0, (score_p_single(anti_from_v(v0)) if v0 is not None else np.inf)
    m = int(n) // 2
    alpha = 2.0 / float(n)
    best_v = np.clip(np.asarray(v0, dtype=np.float64).copy(), 0.0, alpha)
    best_s = score_p_single(anti_from_v(best_v))
    if m == 0 or not np.isfinite(best_s):
        return best_v, np.inf
    maxiter = 45 if n <= 512 else 25
    bounds = [(0.0, float(alpha))] * m
    for bm in (8.0, 16.0, 32.0):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 10.0:
            break
        try:
            res = scipy_opt.minimize(
                smooth_anti_scalar,
                best_v,
                jac=smooth_anti_grad,
                args=(bm,),
                bounds=bounds,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                options={"maxiter": maxiter, "gtol": 1e-7, "ftol": 1e-9},
            )
        except Exception:
            continue
        if res is None or not hasattr(res, "x") or res.x is None:
            continue
        v1 = np.clip(np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64), 0.0, alpha)
        s1 = score_p_single(anti_from_v(v1))
        if np.isfinite(s1) and s1 < best_s:
            best_v = v1
            best_s = s1
    return best_v, best_s


def tiny_binary_anti_search(deadline):
    best_p = None
    best_s = np.inf
    for n in (8, 12, 16, 20, 24):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
            break
        m = n // 2
        cnt = 1 << m
        if cnt > 60000:
            break
        alpha = 2.0 / float(n)
        bits = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
        for mask in range(cnt):
            for i in range(m):
                b = 1 if (mask >> i) & 1 else 0
                bits[i] = b
                bits[n - 1 - i] = 1 - b
            c = np.convolve(bits, bits)
            rmax = int(np.max(c))
            score = 8.0 * rmax / float(n)
            if score < best_s:
                best_s = score
                best_p = alpha * bits.astype(np.float64)
            if score <= 1.0 and n >= 16:
                break
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0:
            break
    return best_p, best_s


def exact_score_sequence(seq):
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
        return float(val)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400.0, **kwargs):
    """Main search entry point. Returns a nonnegative sequence as a list."""
    try:
        seed = int(seed)
    except Exception:
        seed = 42
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0
    budget = max(30.0, float(budget) - 20.0)
    deadline = t0 + budget

    best_p = None
    best_s = 2.0 + 1e-9

    # Seed from supplied SOTA if present.
    try:
        hs = height_sequence_1
        if hs is not None and len(hs) > 0:
            hp = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64)
            hp = np.clip(hp, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if hp.size > 0 and hp.size <= 12000 and np.all(np.isfinite(hp)):
                hs = hp / max(1e-300, float(np.sum(hp)))
                s0 = score_p_single(hs)
                if np.isfinite(s0) and s0 < best_s:
                    best_p = hs
                    best_s = float(s0)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Very short exhaustive anti-binary search.
    if time.monotonic() + 30.0 < deadline:
        p0, s0 = tiny_binary_anti_search(deadline - 30.0)
        if p0 is not None and np.isfinite(s0) and s0 < best_s:
            best_p = p0
            best_s = float(s0)
            print("tiny anti-binary found %.6f" % best_s, flush=True)

    # Main anti-palindromic stages.
    plan = [(256, 0.18), (512, 0.47), (1024, 0.28)]
    for stage_i, (n, frac) in enumerate(plan):
        now = time.monotonic()
        rem = deadline - now
        if rem < 80.0:
            break
        chunk = rem * frac
        chunk = max(60.0, chunk)
        chunk = min(chunk, rem - 20.0)
        n_deadline = now + chunk

        init_vs = []
        if best_p is not None:
            vbest = upsample_v_from_p(best_p, n)
            if vbest is not None and vbest.size == n // 2:
                init_vs.append(vbest)

        if scipy_opt is not None and rem > 160.0:
            vs_sine, ss_sine = sine_anti_search(n, min(n_deadline, now + 70.0), rng)
            if vs_sine is not None and np.isfinite(ss_sine):
                init_vs.append(np.clip(vs_sine, 0.0, 2.0 / float(n)))
                if ss_sine < best_s:
                    best_p = anti_from_v(vs_sine)
                    best_s = float(ss_sine)
                    print("sine DE n=%d score=%.6f" % (n, best_s), flush=True)

        init_vs.extend(power_start_vs(n))

        v, s = population_anti_search(n, n_deadline, rng, init_vs)
        if v is not None and np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s:
            best_p = anti_from_v(v)
            best_s = float(s)
            print("anti population n=%d best=%.6f" % (n, best_s), flush=True)

        if scipy_opt is not None and best_s > (TARGET_SCORE - 0.05) and time.monotonic() + 40.0 < deadline:
            v2, s2 = polish_anti(n, v, min(deadline - 20.0, time.monotonic() + 180.0), rng)
            if v2 is not None and np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_s:
                best_p = anti_from_v(v2)
                best_s = float(s2)
                print("polish anti n=%d best=%.6f" % (n, best_s), flush=True)

        # A short general (non-anti) search after the first meaningful stage.
        if best_s > (TARGET_SCORE + 0.01) and time.monotonic() + 120.0 < deadline:
            init_ps = []
            if best_p is not None:
                init_ps.append(interpolate_sequence(best_p, 512 if n <= 512 else n))
            pg, sg = general_population_search(
                512 if n <= 512 else n,
                min(deadline - 20.0, time.monotonic() + 240.0),
                rng,
                init_ps,
            )
            if pg is not None and np.isfinite(sg) and sg < best_s:
                best_p = pg
                best_s = float(sg)
                print("general search best=%.6f" % best_s, flush=True)

    # If still above target and time remains, try a larger anti sequence.
    if best_s > TARGET_SCORE - 0.04 and time.monotonic() + 180.0 < deadline:
        n = 2048
        init_vs = []
        if best_p is not None:
            vbest = upsample_v_from_p(best_p, n)
            if vbest is not None and vbest.size == n // 2:
                init_vs.append(vbest)
        init_vs.extend(power_start_vs(n)[:8])
        v, s = population_anti_search(
            n,
            min(deadline - 30.0, time.monotonic() + 420.0),
            rng,
            init_vs,
        )
        if v is not None and np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s:
            best_p = anti_from_v(v)
            best_s = float(s)
            print("large anti n=2048 best=%.6f" % best_s, flush=True)

        if scipy_opt is not None and best_s > (TARGET_SCORE - 0.05) and time.monotonic() + 40.0 < deadline:
            v2, s2 = polish_anti(n, v, min(deadline - 20.0, time.monotonic() + 240.0), rng)
            if v2 is not None and np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_s:
                best_p = anti_from_v(v2)
                best_s = float(s2)
                print("large polish best=%.6f" % best_s, flush=True)

    if best_p is None:
        best_p = np.full(256, 1.0 / 256.0, dtype=np.float64)

    # Final cleanup.
    best_p = np.asarray(best_p, dtype=np.float64)
    best_p = np.nan_to_num(best_p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    best_p = np.clip(best_p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(best_p))
    if s <= 0.01:
        best_p = np.full(256, 1.0 / 256.0, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        # Keep the original scale; the evaluator is scale-invariant, but any
        # positive scale below the 1000 clamp is fine.  Normalizing makes the
        # output compact and robust.
        best_p = best_p / s

    seq = [float(x) for x in best_p.tolist()]

    # One final official validation when length is reasonable.
    if len(seq) <= 10000:
        exact = exact_score_sequence(seq)
        if np.isfinite(exact):
            best_s = float(exact)
        else:
            # If official evaluator rejects it, return a safe uniform sequence.
            seq = [float(x) for x in np.full(256, 1.0 / 256.0).tolist()]

    return seq
```
